| S-00132 | phase_studio\app.py:7798 /  | literal | Superflip runs the standard charge-flipping cycle every cycle (unchanged default). '1st Superflip, then SharpED (beta)' runs Superflip only once, then each cycle deblurs with SharpED, reads phi_calc by FFT from that SharpED map for every measured hkl, and recomposes a map from \|Fobs\| + phi_calc for the next cycle; it does not work well with some models. 'SharpED (experimental)' skips Superflip entirely and starts cycle 1 from \|Fobs\| with independent random phases; it does not work yet and is intended for development, not production use. | S-00258 | phase_studio\app.py:8909 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_basic_help_tab | self._add_help_section | <br>            <h3>1. Select the input</h3><br>            <p>Phase Studio can use a Jana2020 <b>.inflip</b> file, a Jana2020 .inflip file with selected external overrides, or an external HKL file.</p><br>            <p><b>Crystal metadata</b> independently selects the authoritative unit cell, space group and composition: Jana2020 .inflip, the selected reference structure, or validated manual input. External HKL data therefore do not require a CIF when complete manual metadata are supplied.</p><br>            <p>For external reflections, select the exact HKL column order first. Use <b>Validate HKL</b> to verify how columns were parsed and <b>Analyze completeness</b> to inspect completeness and data quality before reconstruction.</p><br>            <h3>2. Choose a reconstruction preset</h3><br>            <p><b>Workflow preset</b> (Basic &rarr; Workflow) applies a bundle of starting values in one step; every value stays individually editable afterward. Use it as a starting point, then adjust parameters only when necessary.</p><br>            <ul><li><b>Recommended</b> (default): a general-purpose baseline that matches the built-in defaults.</li><br>            <li><b>small molecule:</b> settings intended as a starting point for small-molecule data.</li><br>            <li><b>inorganic:</b> settings intended as a starting point for inorganic materials.</li><br>            <li><b>MOF atomic resolution:</b> settings intended as a starting point for atomic-resolution framework data.</li><br>            <li><b>MOF medium resolution:</b> settings intended as a starting point for medium-resolution framework data.</li><br>            <li><b>Custom:</b> applies nothing; a placeholder for manually configured values.</li></ul><br>            <p>Applying a preset changes multiple controls. Review the resulting values and adapt them to the dataset; a preset is not a universal scientific recommendation.</p><br>            <p><b>Phasing method</b> defaults to the standard Superflip cycle. Two SharpED phase-recycling methods, one beta and one experimental, are hidden by default; enable <b>Show beta and experimental features</b> on Advanced &rarr; Setup to make them selectable.</p><br>            <h3>3. Configure the iterative workflow</h3><br>            <p><b>observed reflections &rarr; Superflip &rarr; XPLOR map &rarr; EDMA and/or SharpED &rarr; deblurred XPLOR &rarr; EDMA &rarr; next-cycle model</b></p><br>            <p>The exact branches depend on <b>Basic &rarr; Workflow &rarr; Optional processing</b>. The next-cycle source can be Superflip XPLOR, SharpED/deblurred XPLOR, deblurred EDMA CIF, or <b>none</b>. Selecting none forces a one-cycle run.</p><br>            <h3>4. Inspect each cycle</h3><br>            <p>Review convergence metrics, structure previews, detected atom/peak counts, reference agreement when available, Superflip versus SharpED results, and the execution log. Phase Studio does not replace final crystallographic refinement.</p><br>            <h3>5. Return the selected result to Jana2020</h3><br>            <p>After a successful Jana2020 run, <b>Pass to Jana2020</b> opens the result selector with the profile-aware recommendation preselected. In standalone use, <b>Save map and model</b> opens the same selector. Final interpretation and refinement remain in Jana2020.</p><br>         | See enclosing implementation; individual semantic review pending | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| S-00263 | phase_studio\app.py:8935 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_basic_help_tab | self._add_help_section | <br>            <h3>Data input</h3><br>            <p><b>Input mode</b> chooses the reflection source: <b>Jana2020 .inflip</b>, <b>Jana2020 .inflip with overrides</b>, or <b>External HKL</b>. In Jana modes, the .inflip file's fbegin/endf block is the default HKL source and its cell/space-group/composition keywords can provide crystallographic metadata; External mode uses reflection data loaded independently of Jana2020 and requires metadata from another source.</p><br>            <p><b>Jana2020 .inflip file</b> is the Superflip input file used by the two Jana input modes.</p><br>            <p><b>External HKL file</b> replaces only the fbegin/endf block in override mode, or is the required reflection source in external mode.</p><br>            <p><b>HKL format</b> is the exact displayed column order. <b>Set from .inflip</b> imports Jana's dataformat. For example, <b>h k l · F · phase · σ(F)</b> means Miller indices, amplitude, phase in degrees, and amplitude uncertainty. FWHM formats contain peak width rather than measurement uncertainty, so validation and completeness show intensity and peak width separately; no uncertainty-based significance is inferred.</p><br>            <p><b>Validate HKL</b> parses the selected HKL or .inflip reflection block and shows which h, k, l, value, sigma and phase fields were read. <b>Analyze completeness</b> opens completeness and data-statistics plots (d<sub>min</sub>, resolution-dependent completeness, mean I/&sigma;(I)) for the selected data.</p><br>            <h3>Crystal metadata</h3><br>            <p><b>Metadata source</b> is the authoritative source for unit cell, space group and composition: <b>Jana2020 .inflip</b>, the selected <b>Reference file</b>, or <b>Manual</b> entry. Phase Studio does not silently combine metadata from more than one source.</p><br>            <p>When <b>Manual</b> is selected: <b>a, b, c</b> (&Aring;) and <b>alpha, beta, gamma</b> (&deg;) are the unit-cell parameters; <b>Number</b> (1-230) or <b>Symbol</b> identifies the space group; <b>Composition</b> uses Superflip syntax, for example <code>Ag196 S108 O40 B1000</code>.</p><br>            <h3>Reference and initial model</h3><br>            <p><b>Reference file</b> is an optional external reference: CIF/INS/RES files can supply metadata and atom sites used for comparison metrics; Jana/XPLOR/CCP4 maps can serve as a Superflip reference density (referencefile keyword), which also anchors the reciprocal-space origin from cycle 2 onward when no explicit reference is chosen.</p><br>            <p><b>Initial model (cycle 1)</b> is an optional external density or structure model that seeds the first Superflip cycle. Supported inputs are XPLOR, CCP4 and CIF.</p><br>         | See enclosing implementation; individual semantic review pending | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| S-00266 | phase_studio\app.py:8950 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_basic_help_tab | self._add_help_section | <br>            <h3>Reconstruction</h3><br>            <p><b>Workflow preset</b> applies a bundle of starting values in one step; every value stays individually editable afterward. <b>Recommended</b> (default) is a general-purpose baseline matching the built-in defaults; the MOF/small-molecule/inorganic presets tune values for a specific sample type; <b>Custom</b> applies nothing.</p><br>            <p><b>Cycles</b> is the number of iterative Superflip &rarr; EDMA &rarr; SharpED &rarr; EDMA cycles to run. The effective count is forced to 1 when Next-cycle model is <code>none</code>.</p><br>            <p><b>Phasing method</b> chooses the reconstruction algorithm:</p><br>            <ul><br>                <li><b>Superflip</b> (default) &mdash; the standard charge-flipping cycle: Superflip runs every cycle, seeded by Next-cycle model.</li><br>                <li><b>1st Superflip, then SharpED (beta)</b> &mdash; Superflip runs only once, on cycle 1. Every following cycle deblurs the previous map with SharpED, calculates phases by FFT from that deblurred map for every measured reflection (expanded over the full space-group symmetry), and recomposes a map from \|Fobs\| with those phases for the next cycle. Does not work well with some models.</li><br>                <li><b>SharpED (experimental)</b> &mdash; the same recycling loop, but skips Superflip entirely: cycle 1 starts from a map synthesized directly from \|Fobs\| with independent random phases. Not production-ready; can take hundreds of cycles to converge, if it converges at all.</li><br>            </ul><br>            <p>Both recycling methods are hidden unless <b>Show beta and experimental features</b> is checked on Advanced &rarr; Setup. Selecting one disables Next-cycle model, XPLOR damping, Symmetrize SharpED map and the per-cycle EDMA checkboxes below it, since the method defines its own next-cycle map; use <b>Run EDMA on final map</b> (Optional processing) instead. Each completed map is assessed with the active three-metric validation profile; the cycle-to-cycle map correlation remains available in the detailed output.</p><br>            <p><b>Next-cycle model</b> (Superflip phasing method only) is the authoritative source for cycle 2 onward: <b>None</b> forces a one-cycle run; <b>Superflip map (XPLOR)</b> cycles without SharpED processing; <b>SharpED map (XPLOR)</b> uses the SharpED-processed XPLOR map; <b>SharpED structure (EDMA CIF)</b> uses the EDMA structure extracted from the SharpED map and ignores XPLOR damping.</p><br>            <p><b>XPLOR damping (1/x)</b> (Superflip phasing method, XPLOR next-cycle models only) is the inverse damping factor: 1.0 means no damping, 0.5 is equivalent to the previous factor 2.0, 0.25 to factor 4.0.</p><br>            <p><b>Excluded atoms</b> removes selected atom labels from CIF modelfiles before the next Superflip cycle (comma/semicolon/whitespace-separated); it does not apply to XPLOR-only model paths.</p><br>            <h3>SharpED model</h3><br>            <p><b>Model</b> is the SharpED server model name sent with every deblurring request; <b>Refresh models</b> fetches the current list from the configured server and updates this selector. Server URL and API token are on Advanced &rarr; Setup; elements, output resolution, map value exponent and network/upload settings are on Advanced &rarr; SharpED.</p><br>            <h3>Optional processing</h3><br>            <p>Under <b>Superflip cycle</b> (used when Phasing method is Superflip):</p><br>            <ul><br>                <li><b>Run EDMA on Superflip map</b> &mdash; peak-search the Superflip XPLOR map and export CIF/XYZ/PDB.</li><br>                <li><b>Run SharpED</b> &mdash; process the Superflip map with the SharpED server. If disabled, the SharpED map used downstream is just a copy of the Superflip map.</li><br>                <li><b>Symmetrize SharpED map with Superflip (beta)</b> &mdash; after SharpED processing, run Superflip in symmetry mode (no charge flipping) with the SharpED map as modelfile, averaging it according to the supplied space-group symmetry. Hidden unless beta/experimental features are enabled.</li><br>                <li><b>Run EDMA on SharpED map</b> &mdash; peak-search the SharpED (or symmetrized) XPLOR map and export CIF/XYZ/PDB.</li><br>                <li><b>Compute OMIT validation maps (5% holdout)</b> &mdash; freezes a symmetry/Friedel-orbit-safe holdout before cycle 1 and excludes it from every work-data and feedback step. Enables the cross-validation assessment profiles and OMIT map correlation. Roughly doubles Superflip/SharpED time per cycle.</li><br>                <li><b>Calculate R_free on the 5% holdout</b> &mdash; is selected automatically with the option above and reports R_free from reflections never admitted to work data. Recommendation follows the active profile's explicit three-metric priority without a combined score.</li><br>            </ul><br>            <p>Under <b>Phase-recycling methods</b> (used by the two beta/experimental Phasing methods):</p><br>            <ul><li><b>Run EDMA on final map</b> &mdash; run EDMA once, on the last cycle's recomposed \|Fobs\|+phi_calc map.</li></ul><br>         | See enclosing implementation; individual semantic review pending | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| S-00269 | phase_studio\app.py:8980 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_basic_help_tab | self._add_help_section | <br>            <h3>Output</h3><br>            <p><b>Working directory</b> is where Phase Studio writes generated HKL files, Superflip inputs, maps, EDMA results, logs and metrics. Use a dedicated directory per run when reproducibility matters.</p><br>            <p><b>Map format</b> adds one extra saved output on top of the XPLOR map that is always kept internally for EDMA, SharpED and Superflip: <code>ccp4</code> or <code>jana</code> save an extra CCP4 map or Jana m80/m81 density+reflection files; <b>HKL reflections with phases</b> and <b>ShelX (fcf)</b> instead save, for each cycle's Superflip map, the observed reflections (h k l, intensity/F&sup2;, sigma) together with phases &mdash; and, for ShelX, calculated F&sup2; &mdash; read by FFT from that map, as a standardized text file or a ShelX/Jana-compatible .fcf file, in place of an extra density map.</p><br>            <p><b>Structure format</b> adds one extra saved structure format on top of the CIF that is always kept internally for metrics and next-cycle modelfiles: <code>xyz</code> or <code>pdb</code>.</p><br>         | See enclosing implementation; individual semantic review pending | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| S-00272 | phase_studio\app.py:8987 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_basic_help_tab | self._add_help_section | f"""<br>            <p>Each of the three mechanisms below has its own <b>Enable</b> checkbox at the top of its group; unchecking it grays out the rest of that group and skips the mechanism entirely. <b>Start after cycle</b> keeps its own 1-999 range regardless of the current <b>Cycles</b> value (Basic &rarr; Workflow), so it can be set up ahead of raising Cycles; the fields below it stay grayed out with a hint whenever the current Cycles value cannot reach the configured starting cycle.</p><br>            <h3>Missing-reflection completion</h3><br>            <p><b>Start after cycle</b> is the first completed cycle whose map is used to add missing reflections for the next cycle. <b>Maximum added reflections (%)</b> caps generated missing reflections as a percent of the current reflection count, preventing feedback from overwhelming measured data.</p><br>            <h3>Intensity correction</h3><br>            <p><b>Start after cycle</b> is the first completed cycle whose map is used to damp observed intensities for the next cycle. <b>Correction damping</b> ranges from 0 (keeps observed values) to 1 (replaces them with scaled map-derived values). <b>I/&sigma;(I) limit</b> selects positive observations strictly below the intensity-significance threshold; amplitudes use the first-order approximation F/[2&sigma;(F)], not recovered experimental intensity significance. Zero bypasses significance filtering for all map-supported reflections. The average intensity change across corrected reflections is plotted on the <b>Intensity correction</b> convergence tab (lower is better).</p><br>            <h3>Powder overlap repartitioning</h3><br>            <p>Only applies to reflections carrying an FWHM value ({html.escape(format_reflection_data_mode(REFLECTION_DATA_MODE_INTENSITY_FWHM))} or {html.escape(format_reflection_data_mode(REFLECTION_DATA_MODE_AMPLITUDE_FWHM))}). <b>Start after cycle</b> is the first completed cycle whose map is used to redistribute overlapping reflections for the next cycle. Reflections whose Bragg peaks overlap in a powder pattern -- delta(2θ) below <b>Separation factor</b> times the mean of their FWHM, Superflip's own <code>fwhmseparation</code> convention -- have their combined observed intensity redistributed between them using intensities calculated by FFT from that cycle's map (the SharpED map when SharpED deblurring is enabled, otherwise a copy of the Superflip map), blended by <b>Map ratio mix</b> (0 keeps the observed split, 1 uses the map split fully; default 1). The group total is always conserved. <b>Wavelength</b> is required to compute 2θ; if left at 0 it is auto-detected first from the loaded <code>.inflip</code> file's <code>lambda</code>/<code>wavelength</code> line, then from the reference file's <code>_diffrn_radiation_wavelength</code> tag -- enter it manually if neither source has it. Each time it runs, a <code>cycle_NNN_powder_repartitioning.log</code> file is written with the number of overlap groups considered, their average size, their average intensity change, and the before/after intensities for every reflection in the 3 groups with the largest d-spacing. The average intensity change per group is also plotted on the <b>Powder repartitioning</b> convergence tab (lower is better).</p><br>        """ | See enclosing implementation; individual semantic review pending | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| S-00412 | phase_studio\app.py:9204 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_advanced_help_tab | self._add_help_section | <br>            <h3>External programs</h3><br>            <p><b>Superflip executable</b> is the absolute path to the scientific executable under the active Jana2020 root (normally <code>SUPERFLIP\superflip.exe</code>).</p><br>            <p><b>EDMA executable</b> is the absolute path to the Jana2020 EDMA executable used for peak extraction and structure export from XPLOR density maps (default <code>C:\Jana2020\SUPERFLIP\EDMA.exe</code>).</p><br>            <p>Superflip and EDMA are external third-party programs, distributed by their own authors and not bundled with Phase Studio. Both are normally installed with Jana2020; if a path is not configured, Phase Studio looks for them in <code>C:\Jana2020\SUPERFLIP</code> when a workflow starts and fills the path in automatically. Official downloads: <a href="https://superflip.fzu.cz/download/superflip_win.zip">superflip_win.zip</a> and <a href="https://superflip.fzu.cz/download/EDMA_win.zip">EDMA_win.zip</a>; license information at <a href="https://superflip.fzu.cz/">superflip.fzu.cz</a>.</p><br>            <p>Requirements are checked immediately before a workflow starts, and only for the stages that workflow actually uses -- a workflow that never runs EDMA is never asked for EDMA.</p><br>            <h3>SharpED connection</h3><br>            <p><b>Server URL</b> is the SharpED inference-server base URL; the documented Public API uses <code>https://jana.fzu.cz/</code>. <b>API token</b> authorizes upload/status/download requests and is never written to logs or error messages.</p><br>            <p>SharpED processing needs an active Jana2020 or SharpED account. Sign in at <a href="https://sharped.fzu.cz/sharp-ed">sharped.fzu.cz/sharp-ed</a> and create a token with <b>Create token</b>, then paste it into <b>API token</b> above.</p><br>            <h3>Interface</h3><br>            <p><b>Show beta and experimental features</b> is unchecked by default. While off, the beta/experimental Phasing methods and Symmetrize SharpED map with Superflip (beta) are removed from the Basic tabs entirely, not just disabled. | S-00415 | phase_studio\app.py:9217 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_advanced_help_tab | self._add_help_section | <br>            <h3>What Superflip does</h3><br>            <p>Superflip is the density-reconstruction and phase-retrieval stage. Phase Studio prepares reflections, crystallographic metadata and input, executes Superflip, then uses its density map for direct inspection, EDMA peak extraction, SharpED processing and iterative feedback.</p><br>            <h3>Calculation</h3><br>            <p><b>Algorithm</b> maps to Superflip's <code>perform</code> keyword; common values are <code>CF</code> (normal charge-flipping, used by the presets), <code>lde</code>, <code>general</code>, <code>fourier</code> and <code>symmetry</code>; <code>AAR</code> is kept for executables that support it.</p><br>            <p><b>Maximum iterations</b> limits one Superflip run. <b>Repeat mode</b> controls repeated independent attempts and convergence sampling. <b>Random seed</b> initializes random numbers; use a fixed value for reproducibility or Superflip's automatic syntax. <b>Delta</b> (<code>AUTO</code> lets Superflip estimate the flip threshold), <b>Weak ratio</b> and <b>Biso</b> (overall isotropic B-factor used to sharpen the map; 0.000 disables sharpening) are advanced parameters normally left at preset/default values. <b>Enable final polish</b> adds <code>polish yes</code>, activating Superflip's final polishing stage when supported.</p><br>            <h3>Density / solution selection</h3><br>            <p><b>Voxel grid</b> is the Superflip <code>voxel</code> keyword: blank omits it; three integers set an explicit grid; <code>AUTO</code> computes a 0.2 &Aring; grid from the unit cell.</p><br>            <p><b>Stored best densities</b> and <b>Density selection metric</b> are the two arguments of <code>bestdensities</code> &mdash; how many best density maps Superflip keeps, and whether it selects by rvalue, peakiness, symmetry or reference agreement. Selecting <b>symmetry</b> biases selection toward symmetry-consistent solutions.</p><br>            <p><b>Search symmetry</b> maps to <code>searchsymmetry</code> (<code>no</code>, <code>shift</code> or <code>average</code>). <b>Derive symmetry</b> maps to <code>derivesymmetry</code> (commonly <code>yes</code>, <code>no</code> or <code>use</code>, depending on the Superflip version).</p><br>            <h3>Reflection handling</h3><br>            <p><b>Minimum observed value/&sigma;</b> filters weak observed reflections before writing the Superflip HKL block. <b>High-resolution cutoff d<sub>min</sub> (&Aring;)</b> is an optional resolution cutoff; 0 keeps all reflections. <b>Normalization</b> is an optional reflection-normalization keyword (<code>none</code> is the safest default for the Windows executable). <b>Resolution shells</b> is used only when a supported normalization keyword is written. <b>Missing reflections</b> supplies Superflip's <code>missing</code> line, for example bounds for missing reflections. <b>Electrons</b> is Superflip's <code>electrons</code> keyword; leave blank to omit it.</p><br>            <h3>Reference density</h3><br>            <p>The <code>referencefile</code> keyword is written automatically: from the selected Reference file when one is chosen on Basic &rarr; Input, or from cycle 2 onward when none is selected, using the previous cycle's EDMA CIF (or its XPLOR map if EDMA produced no usable peaks). This keeps Superflip's origin fixed in reciprocal space between cycles. <code>dataitemwidths</code> is unnecessary and not exposed, because Phase Studio always writes whitespace-separated fbegin/endf records.</p><br>            <h3>Additional keywords</h3><br>            <p><b>Extra Superflip keywords</b> lets expert users append documented keyword lines, inserted before <code>fbegin</code>, that are not represented by a dedicated control. <b>Load settings from .inflip</b> reads Superflip keyword settings from an existing .inflip file (reflection data blocks are ignored).</p><br>            <h3>Output</h3><br>            <p>XPLOR (map) and CIF (structure) are always produced internally because EDMA, SharpED and later-cycle modelfiles consume them; the <b>Map format</b> and <b>Structure format</b> choices on Basic &rarr; Output add one extra saved format on top for external inspection, molecular-graphics viewers or Jana2020.</p><br>         | See enclosing implementation; individual semantic review pending | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| S-00420 | phase_studio\app.py:9238 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_advanced_help_tab | self._add_help_section | <br>            <h3>What EDMA does in Phase Studio</h3><br>            <p>EDMA extracts density maxima from an XPLOR map. Phase Studio uses these maxima to create structural models and exports, independently for the Superflip map and the SharpED map when enabled.</p><br>            <h3>Peak extraction</h3><br>            <p><b>Superflip threshold</b> and <b>SharpED threshold (&sigma;)</b> are multipliers of the corresponding map sigma; Phase Studio converts each multiplier to EDMA's absolute <code>plimit</code> for that map. A higher threshold is stricter; a lower threshold includes more maxima &mdash; there is no universal correct value. <b>Maxima selection = all</b> requests all maxima above plimit; advanced users may enter more restrictive documented EDMA syntax. <b>Atom-count mode = composition</b> requests atom counts consistent with the chemical composition.</p><br>            <h3>Symmetry and peak positions</h3><br>            <p><b>Merge distance</b> is the tolerance (&Aring;) used when reducing maxima to one representative per full space-group orbit for the CIF asymmetric unit. <b>Full-cell = no</b> requests symmetry-independent maxima when the supplied symmetry is correct; <code>yes</code> lists the full unit cell. <b>Use center of charge</b> refines peak positions to the center of charge of each density basin before export.</p><br>            <h3>Chemical filtering</h3><br>            <p><b>Charge limit</b> is the minimum integrated charge for exported maxima; useful for suppressing noise peaks in rough or over-sharpened maps. <b>Charge-list threshold</b> supplies EDMA's <code>chlimlist</code> setting, for example <code>0.0057 relative</code>, used together with atom-count/composition-based export.</p><br>            <h3>Additional keywords</h3><br>            <p><b>Extra EDMA keywords</b> appends documented EDMA options not represented by a dedicated control.</p><br>         | See enclosing implementation; individual semantic review pending | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| S-00425 | phase_studio\app.py:9252 / IterativeSuperflipPipelineQtGUI._build_advanced_help_tab | self._add_help_section | <br>            <h3>What SharpED does</h3><br>            <p>SharpED processes and deblurs the XPLOR density map from Superflip. After EDMA extraction the result can be inspected in Structure Comparison, used for EDMA, optionally symmetrized, used as a next-cycle XPLOR model, or handed to Jana2020. If server processing is disabled, the workflow continues without a genuinely processed SharpED result.</p><br>            <p><b>Model</b> selection is on Basic &rarr; Workflow. Server connection is on Advanced &rarr; Setup (server URL/API token); elements, output resolution, map value exponent and upload/network settings are on Advanced &rarr; SharpED (everything else below).</p><br>            <h3>1. Server connection</h3><br>            <p><b>Server URL</b> is the inference-server address. <b>API token</b> authenticates server requests. Obtain a token from the SharpED project and API-token page.</p><br>            <h3>2. Model and elements</h3><br>            <p><b>Model</b> (Basic &rarr; Workflow) is sent to the server; <b>Refresh models</b> updates the selector; <code>default</code> uses the server default. <b>Elements</b> are sent to SharpED; when blank, Phase Studio derives unique non-hydrogen elements from the reference composition.</p><br>            <h3>3. Output resolution</h3><br>            <p><b>Output resolution (&Aring;)</b> is the requested sampling/resolution of the SharpED density map.</p><br>            <h3>4. Map value exponent</h3><br>            <p><b>Map value exponent</b> <i>a</i> applies a reversible signed power transform to the voxel values of the map sent to SharpED, and the exact inverse transform to the map SharpED returns. Forward: <code>sign(x)&middot;\|x\|<sup>a</sup></code>. Inverse: <code>sign(x)&middot;\|x\|<sup>1/a</sup></code>. The transform is signed because density maps contain negative voxel values, for which a plain <code>x<sup>a</sup></code> is not real-valued.</p><br>            <p>Qualitatively, <i>a</i> &lt; 1 compresses the dynamic range of the absolute map values, <i>a</i> = 1 (the default) leaves the map unchanged, and <i>a</i> &gt; 1 expands the dynamic range. <i>a</i> = 0 disables the transform entirely &mdash; <code>x<sup>0</sup></code> discards the magnitude and cannot be inverted, so it is treated as a bypass rather than as a transform.</p><br>            <p>Only voxel values are affected. Grid, origin, cell, symmetry, map dimensions, map format, metadata and the SharpED request parameters are all unchanged, and everything downstream of SharpED (EDMA, symmetrization, metrics, phase recycling, visualization, export and the Jana2020 hand-off) always sees values in the original map-value domain. No normalization, shifting, clipping or rescaling of any kind is added.</p><br>            <p>This is an experimental preprocessing option offered for exploration. Phase Studio makes no claim that any particular exponent improves crystallographic results; the default of 1.000 reproduces the standard workflow exactly.</p><br>            <h3>5. Upload and network</h3><br>            <p><b>Upload limit</b> checks XPLOR size locally; its application default is 100 MB and 0 disables this local check (confirm the actual limit with the configured service). If Voxel grid is empty/omit, Phase Studio can add a coarser Superflip voxel keyword before map calculation so the native Superflip XPLOR fits under this limit. <b>HTTP timeout</b> covers model queries, upload, status and download requests and is enforced at 600 seconds minimum. <b>Polling interval</b> sets the delay between status checks. <b>Maximum polls</b> limits those checks; <b>-1</b> means no fixed polling limit.</p><br>            <h3>6. SharpED in iterative workflows</h3><br>            <p><b>Run SharpED</b> (Basic &rarr; Workflow &rarr; Optional processing) enables server processing; if disabled, the SharpED map used downstream is a copy of the Superflip map. <b>Symmetrize SharpED map with Superflip (beta)</b> performs symmetry averaging, not another charge-flipping reconstruction. Next-cycle model's <b>SharpED map (XPLOR)</b> option feeds the SharpED map into the next cycle; <b>SharpED structure (EDMA CIF)</b> feeds its EDMA structure instead.</p><br>            <h3>7. Phase-recycling methods (beta/experimental)</h3><br>            <p><b>1st Superflip, then SharpED (beta)</b> and <b>SharpED (experimental)</b> use SharpED for phase recycling instead of iterative Superflip cycling. They are hidden from the Phasing method list by default; check <b>Show beta and experimental features</b> (Advanced &rarr; Setup) to select them. Neither is production-ready.</p><br>         | See enclosing implementation; individual semantic review pending | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| R-00087 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h3>External programs</h3><br>            <p><b>Superflip executable</b> is the absolute path to the scientific executable under the active Jana2020 root (normally <code>SUPERFLIP\superflip.exe</code>).</p><br>            <p><b>EDMA executable</b> is the absolute path to the Jana2020 EDMA executable used for peak extraction and structure export from XPLOR density maps (default <code>C:\Jana2020\SUPERFLIP\EDMA.exe</code>).</p><br>            <p>Superflip and EDMA are external third-party programs, distributed by their own authors and not bundled with Phase Studio. Both are normally installed with Jana2020; if a path is not configured, Phase Studio looks for them in <code>C:\Jana2020\SUPERFLIP</code> when a workflow starts and fills the path in automatically. Official downloads: <a href="https://superflip.fzu.cz/download/superflip_win.zip">superflip_win.zip</a> and <a href="https://superflip.fzu.cz/download/EDMA_win.zip">EDMA_win.zip</a>; license information at <a href="https://superflip.fzu.cz/">superflip.fzu.cz</a>.</p><br>            <p>Requirements are checked immediately before a workflow starts, and only for the stages that workflow actually uses -- a workflow that never runs EDMA is never asked for EDMA.</p><br>            <h3>SharpED connection</h3><br>            <p><b>Server URL</b> is the SharpED inference-server base URL; the documented Public API uses <code>https://jana.fzu.cz/</code>. <b>API token</b> authorizes upload/status/download requests and is never written to logs or error messages.</p><br>            <p>SharpED processing needs an active Jana2020 or SharpED account. Sign in at <a href="https://sharped.fzu.cz/sharp-ed">sharped.fzu.cz/sharp-ed</a> and create a token with <b>Create token</b>, then paste it into <b>API token</b> above.</p><br>            <h3>Interface</h3><br>            <p><b>Show beta and experimental features</b> is unchecked by default. While off, the beta/experimental Phasing methods and Symmetrize SharpED map with Superflip (beta) are removed from the Basic tabs entirely, not just disabled. Enable it to make them selectable; turning it off again while one is active falls back to standard Superflip.</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00088 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QToolButton text | ↑ Contents | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00089 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QGroupBox title | Superflip guide | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00090 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h3>What Superflip does</h3><br>            <p>Superflip is the density-reconstruction and phase-retrieval stage. Phase Studio prepares reflections, crystallographic metadata and input, executes Superflip, then uses its density map for direct inspection, EDMA peak extraction, SharpED processing and iterative feedback.</p><br>            <h3>Calculation</h3><br>            <p><b>Algorithm</b> maps to Superflip's <code>perform</code> keyword; common values are <code>CF</code> (normal charge-flipping, used by the presets), <code>lde</code>, <code>general</code>, <code>fourier</code> and <code>symmetry</code>; <code>AAR</code> is kept for executables that support it.</p><br>            <p><b>Maximum iterations</b> limits one Superflip run. <b>Repeat mode</b> controls repeated independent attempts and convergence sampling. <b>Random seed</b> initializes random numbers; use a fixed value for reproducibility or Superflip's automatic syntax. <b>Delta</b> (<code>AUTO</code> lets Superflip estimate the flip threshold), <b>Weak ratio</b> and <b>Biso</b> (overall isotropic B-factor used to sharpen the map; 0.000 disables sharpening) are advanced parameters normally left at preset/default values. <b>Enable final polish</b> adds <code>polish yes</code>, activating Superflip's final polishing stage when supported.</p><br>            <h3>Density / solution selection</h3><br>            <p><b>Voxel grid</b> is the Superflip <code>voxel</code> keyword: blank omits it; three integers set an explicit grid; <code>AUTO</code> computes a 0.2 &Aring; grid from the unit cell.</p><br>            <p><b>Stored best densities</b> and <b>Density selection metric</b> are the two arguments of <code>bestdensities</code> &mdash; how many best density maps Superflip keeps, and whether it selects by rvalue, peakiness, symmetry or reference agreement. Selecting <b>symmetry</b> biases selection toward symmetry-consistent solutions.</p><br>            <p><b>Search symmetry</b> maps to <code>searchsymmetry</code> (<code>no</code>, <code>shift</code> or <code>average</code>). <b>Derive symmetry</b> maps to <code>derivesymmetry</code> (commonly <code>yes</code>, <code>no</code> or <code>use</code>, depending on the Superflip version).</p><br>            <h3>Reflection handling</h3><br>            <p><b>Minimum observed value/&sigma;</b> filters weak observed reflections before writing the Superflip HKL block. <b>High-resolution cutoff d<sub>min</sub> (&Aring;)</b> is an optional resolution cutoff; 0 keeps all reflections. <b>Normalization</b> is an optional reflection-normalization keyword (<code>none</code> is the safest default for the Windows executable). <b>Resolution shells</b> is used only when a supported normalization keyword is written. <b>Missing reflections</b> supplies Superflip's <code>missing</code> line, for example bounds for missing reflections. <b>Electrons</b> is Superflip's <code>electrons</code> keyword; leave blank to omit it.</p><br>            <h3>Reference density</h3><br>            <p>The <code>referencefile</code> keyword is written automatically: from the selected Reference file when one is chosen on Basic &rarr; Input, or from cycle 2 onward when none is selected, using the previous cycle's EDMA CIF (or its XPLOR map if EDMA produced no usable peaks). This keeps Superflip's origin fixed in reciprocal space between cycles. <code>dataitemwidths</code> is unnecessary and not exposed, because Phase Studio always writes whitespace-separated fbegin/endf records.</p><br>            <h3>Additional keywords</h3><br>            <p><b>Extra Superflip keywords</b> lets expert users append documented keyword lines, inserted before <code>fbegin</code>, that are not represented by a dedicated control. <b>Load settings from .inflip</b> reads Superflip keyword settings from an existing .inflip file (reflection data blocks are ignored).</p><br>            <h3>Output</h3><br>            <p>XPLOR (map) and CIF (structure) are always produced internally because EDMA, SharpED and later-cycle modelfiles consume them; the <b>Map format</b> and <b>Structure format</b> choices on Basic &rarr; Output add one extra saved format on top for external inspection, molecular-graphics viewers or Jana2020.</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00091 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <b>Starting point</b><br>CF and the supplied preset values are initial settings only; choose parameters appropriate for the dataset and intended method. | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00092 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QToolButton text | ↑ Contents | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00093 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QGroupBox title | EDMA guide | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00094 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h3>What EDMA does in Phase Studio</h3><br>            <p>EDMA extracts density maxima from an XPLOR map. Phase Studio uses these maxima to create structural models and exports, independently for the Superflip map and the SharpED map when enabled.</p><br>            <h3>Peak extraction</h3><br>            <p><b>Superflip threshold</b> and <b>SharpED threshold (&sigma;)</b> are multipliers of the corresponding map sigma; Phase Studio converts each multiplier to EDMA's absolute <code>plimit</code> for that map. A higher threshold is stricter; a lower threshold includes more maxima &mdash; there is no universal correct value. <b>Maxima selection = all</b> requests all maxima above plimit; advanced users may enter more restrictive documented EDMA syntax. <b>Atom-count mode = composition</b> requests atom counts consistent with the chemical composition.</p><br>            <h3>Symmetry and peak positions</h3><br>            <p><b>Merge distance</b> is the tolerance (&Aring;) used when reducing maxima to one representative per full space-group orbit for the CIF asymmetric unit. <b>Full-cell = no</b> requests symmetry-independent maxima when the supplied symmetry is correct; <code>yes</code> lists the full unit cell. <b>Use center of charge</b> refines peak positions to the center of charge of each density basin before export.</p><br>            <h3>Chemical filtering</h3><br>            <p><b>Charge limit</b> is the minimum integrated charge for exported maxima; useful for suppressing noise peaks in rough or over-sharpened maps. <b>Charge-list threshold</b> supplies EDMA's <code>chlimlist</code> setting, for example <code>0.0057 relative</code>, used together with atom-count/composition-based export.</p><br>            <h3>Additional keywords</h3><br>            <p><b>Extra EDMA keywords</b> appends documented EDMA options not represented by a dedicated control.</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00095 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <b>Important</b><br>Select each EDMA threshold for its map and assess the resulting peaks; there is no universal threshold. | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| R-00097 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QGroupBox title | SharpED guide | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00098 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h3>What SharpED does</h3><br>            <p>SharpED processes and deblurs the XPLOR density map from Superflip. After EDMA extraction the result can be inspected in Structure Comparison, used for EDMA, optionally symmetrized, used as a next-cycle XPLOR model, or handed to Jana2020. If server processing is disabled, the workflow continues without a genuinely processed SharpED result.</p><br>            <p><b>Model</b> selection is on Basic &rarr; Workflow. Server connection is on Advanced &rarr; Setup (server URL/API token); elements, output resolution, map value exponent and upload/network settings are on Advanced &rarr; SharpED (everything else below).</p><br>            <h3>1. Server connection</h3><br>            <p><b>Server URL</b> is the inference-server address. <b>API token</b> authenticates server requests. Obtain a token from the SharpED project and API-token page.</p><br>            <h3>2. Model and elements</h3><br>            <p><b>Model</b> (Basic &rarr; Workflow) is sent to the server; <b>Refresh models</b> updates the selector; <code>default</code> uses the server default. <b>Elements</b> are sent to SharpED; when blank, Phase Studio derives unique non-hydrogen elements from the reference composition.</p><br>            <h3>3. Output resolution</h3><br>            <p><b>Output resolution (&Aring;)</b> is the requested sampling/resolution of the SharpED density map.</p><br>            <h3>4. Map value exponent</h3><br>            <p><b>Map value exponent</b> <i>a</i> applies a reversible signed power transform to the voxel values of the map sent to SharpED, and the exact inverse transform to the map SharpED returns. Forward: <code>sign(x)&middot;\|x\|<sup>a</sup></code>. Inverse: <code>sign(x)&middot;\|x\|<sup>1/a</sup></code>. The transform is signed because density maps contain negative voxel values, for which a plain <code>x<sup>a</sup></code> is not real-valued.</p><br>            <p>Qualitatively, <i>a</i> &lt; 1 compresses the dynamic range of the absolute map values, <i>a</i> = 1 (the default) leaves the map unchanged, and <i>a</i> &gt; 1 expands the dynamic range. <i>a</i> = 0 disables the transform entirely &mdash; <code>x<sup>0</sup></code> discards the magnitude and cannot be inverted, so it is treated as a bypass rather than as a transform.</p><br>            <p>Only voxel values are affected. Grid, origin, cell, symmetry, map dimensions, map format, metadata and the SharpED request parameters are all unchanged, and everything downstream of SharpED (EDMA, symmetrization, metrics, phase recycling, visualization, export and the Jana2020 hand-off) always sees values in the original map-value domain. No normalization, shifting, clipping or rescaling of any kind is added.</p><br>            <p>This is an experimental preprocessing option offered for exploration. Phase Studio makes no claim that any particular exponent improves crystallographic results; the default of 1.000 reproduces the standard workflow exactly.</p><br>            <h3>5. Upload and network</h3><br>            <p><b>Upload limit</b> checks XPLOR size locally; its application default is 100 MB and 0 disables this local check (confirm the actual limit with the configured service). If Voxel grid is empty/omit, Phase Studio can add a coarser Superflip voxel keyword before map calculation so the native Superflip XPLOR fits under this limit. <b>HTTP timeout</b> covers model queries, upload, status and download requests and is enforced at 600 seconds minimum. <b>Polling interval</b> sets the delay between status checks. <b>Maximum polls</b> limits those checks; <b>-1</b> means no fixed polling limit.</p><br>            <h3>6. SharpED in iterative workflows</h3><br>            <p><b>Run SharpED</b> (Basic &rarr; Workflow &rarr; Optional processing) enables server processing; if disabled, the SharpED map used downstream is a copy of the Superflip map. <b>Symmetrize SharpED map with Superflip (beta)</b> performs symmetry averaging, not another charge-flipping reconstruction. Next-cycle model's <b>SharpED map (XPLOR)</b> option feeds the SharpED map into the next cycle; <b>SharpED structure (EDMA CIF)</b> feeds its EDMA structure instead.</p><br>            <h3>7. Phase-recycling methods (beta/experimental)</h3><br>            <p><b>1st Superflip, then SharpED (beta)</b> and <b>SharpED (experimental)</b> use SharpED for phase recycling instead of iterative Superflip cycling. They are hidden from the Phasing method list by default; check <b>Show beta and experimental features</b> (Advanced &rarr; Setup) to select them. Neither is production-ready.</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| R-00105 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <p style="font-family: Cascadia Mono, Consolas, monospace; color: #2264b8;">Superflip keyword quick reference<br><br>Structure and symmetry, generated from Reference CIF:<br>  title, dimension, cell, spacegroup, centro, centers/endcenters,<br>  symmetry/endsymmetry, composition.<br><br>Input reflection data:<br>  fbegin/endf, dataformat, dataitemwidths, reflstartline, reflendline,<br>  resunits, lambda.<br><br>Output and model files:<br>  outputfile, outputformat, filebase, rewriteoutput, modelfile,<br>  commandfile, terminal, expandedlog, coverage.<br><br>Density modification and convergence:<br>  perform, maxcycles, repeatmode, bestdensities, delta, weakratio,<br>  Biso, randomseed, polish, addcycles, convergencemode, skipstart.<br><br>Normalization and missing reflections:<br>  normalize, nresshells, missing, electrons.<br><br>Symmetry/origin handling:<br>  searchsymmetry, derivesymmetry, referencesymmetry/endreferencesymmetry,<br>  usephases, viewprogress.<br><br>Grid and higher-dimensional options:<br>  voxel, finevoxel, realdimension, qvectors/endqvectors.<br><br>Histogram/powder-specific options:<br>  histogram/endhistogram, hmparameters, fwhmratio.<br><br>Use Extra Superflip keywords for any manual keyword not exposed as a widget,<br>for example:<br>  expandedlog yes<br>  coverage no<br>  addcycles 200<br>  convergencemode rvalue 30<br>  usephases firstcycle<br></p> | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| R-00359 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h3>1. Select the input</h3><br>            <p>Phase Studio can use a Jana2020 <b>.inflip</b> file, a Jana2020 .inflip file with selected external overrides, or an external HKL file.</p><br>            <p><b>Crystal metadata</b> independently selects the authoritative unit cell, space group and composition: Jana2020 .inflip, the selected reference structure, or validated manual input. External HKL data therefore do not require a CIF when complete manual metadata are supplied.</p><br>            <p>For external reflections, select the exact HKL column order first. Use <b>Validate HKL</b> to verify how columns were parsed and <b>Analyze completeness</b> to inspect completeness and data quality before reconstruction.</p><br>            <h3>2. Choose a reconstruction preset</h3><br>            <p><b>Workflow preset</b> (Basic &rarr; Workflow) applies a bundle of starting values in one step; every value stays individually editable afterward. Use it as a starting point, then adjust parameters only when necessary.</p><br>            <ul><li><b>Recommended</b> (default): a general-purpose baseline that matches the built-in defaults.</li><br>            <li><b>small molecule:</b> settings intended as a starting point for small-molecule data.</li><br>            <li><b>inorganic:</b> settings intended as a starting point for inorganic materials.</li><br>            <li><b>MOF atomic resolution:</b> settings intended as a starting point for atomic-resolution framework data.</li><br>            <li><b>MOF medium resolution:</b> settings intended as a starting point for medium-resolution framework data.</li><br>            <li><b>Custom:</b> applies nothing; a placeholder for manually configured values.</li></ul><br>            <p>Applying a preset changes multiple controls. Review the resulting values and adapt them to the dataset; a preset is not a universal scientific recommendation.</p><br>            <p><b>Phasing method</b> defaults to the standard Superflip cycle. Two SharpED phase-recycling methods, one beta and one experimental, are hidden by default; enable <b>Show beta and experimental features</b> on Advanced &rarr; Setup to make them selectable.</p><br>            <h3>3. Configure the iterative workflow</h3><br>            <p><b>observed reflections &rarr; Superflip &rarr; XPLOR map &rarr; EDMA and/or SharpED &rarr; deblurred XPLOR &rarr; EDMA &rarr; next-cycle model</b></p><br>            <p>The exact branches depend on <b>Basic &rarr; Workflow &rarr; Optional processing</b>. The next-cycle source can be Superflip XPLOR, SharpED/deblurred XPLOR, deblurred EDMA CIF, or <b>none</b>. Selecting none forces a one-cycle run.</p><br>            <h3>4. Inspect each cycle</h3><br>            <p>Review convergence metrics, structure previews, detected atom/peak counts, reference agreement when available, Superflip versus SharpED results, and the execution log. Phase Studio does not replace final crystallographic refinement.</p><br>            <h3>5. Return the selected result to Jana2020</h3><br>            <p>After a successful Jana2020 run, <b>Pass to Jana2020</b> opens the result selector with the profile-aware recommendation preselected. In standalone use, <b>Save map and model</b> opens the same selector. Final interpretation and refinement remain in Jana2020.</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00360 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | WorkflowDiagram toolTip | Optional branches depend on the Workflow settings. | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| R-00364 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h3>Data input</h3><br>            <p><b>Input mode</b> chooses the reflection source: <b>Jana2020 .inflip</b>, <b>Jana2020 .inflip with overrides</b>, or <b>External HKL</b>. In Jana modes, the .inflip file's fbegin/endf block is the default HKL source and its cell/space-group/composition keywords can provide crystallographic metadata; External mode uses reflection data loaded independently of Jana2020 and requires metadata from another source.</p><br>            <p><b>Jana2020 .inflip file</b> is the Superflip input file used by the two Jana input modes.</p><br>            <p><b>External HKL file</b> replaces only the fbegin/endf block in override mode, or is the required reflection source in external mode.</p><br>            <p><b>HKL format</b> is the exact displayed column order. <b>Set from .inflip</b> imports Jana's dataformat. For example, <b>h k l · F · phase · σ(F)</b> means Miller indices, amplitude, phase in degrees, and amplitude uncertainty. FWHM formats contain peak width rather than measurement uncertainty, so validation and completeness show intensity and peak width separately; no uncertainty-based significance is inferred.</p><br>            <p><b>Validate HKL</b> parses the selected HKL or .inflip reflection block and shows which h, k, l, value, sigma and phase fields were read. <b>Analyze completeness</b> opens completeness and data-statistics plots (d<sub>min</sub>, resolution-dependent completeness, mean I/&sigma;(I)) for the selected data.</p><br>            <h3>Crystal metadata</h3><br>            <p><b>Metadata source</b> is the authoritative source for unit cell, space group and composition: <b>Jana2020 .inflip</b>, the selected <b>Reference file</b>, or <b>Manual</b> entry. Phase Studio does not silently combine metadata from more than one source.</p><br>            <p>When <b>Manual</b> is selected: <b>a, b, c</b> (&Aring;) and <b>alpha, beta, gamma</b> (&deg;) are the unit-cell parameters; <b>Number</b> (1-230) or <b>Symbol</b> identifies the space group; <b>Composition</b> uses Superflip syntax, for example <code>Ag196 S108 O40 B1000</code>.</p><br>            <h3>Reference and initial model</h3><br>            <p><b>Reference file</b> is an optional external reference: CIF/INS/RES files can supply metadata and atom sites used for comparison metrics; Jana/XPLOR/CCP4 maps can serve as a Superflip reference density (referencefile keyword), which also anchors the reciprocal-space origin from cycle 2 onward when no explicit reference is chosen.</p><br>            <p><b>Initial model (cycle 1)</b> is an optional external density or structure model that seeds the first Superflip cycle. Supported inputs are XPLOR, CCP4 and CIF.</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00365 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QToolButton text | ↑ Contents | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00366 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QGroupBox title | Workflow reference | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00367 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h3>Reconstruction</h3><br>            <p><b>Workflow preset</b> applies a bundle of starting values in one step; every value stays individually editable afterward. <b>Recommended</b> (default) is a general-purpose baseline matching the built-in defaults; the MOF/small-molecule/inorganic presets tune values for a specific sample type; <b>Custom</b> applies nothing.</p><br>            <p><b>Cycles</b> is the number of iterative Superflip &rarr; EDMA &rarr; SharpED &rarr; EDMA cycles to run. The effective count is forced to 1 when Next-cycle model is <code>none</code>.</p><br>            <p><b>Phasing method</b> chooses the reconstruction algorithm:</p><br>            <ul><br>                <li><b>Superflip</b> (default) &mdash; the standard charge-flipping cycle: Superflip runs every cycle, seeded by Next-cycle model.</li><br>                <li><b>1st Superflip, then SharpED (beta)</b> &mdash; Superflip runs only once, on cycle 1. Every following cycle deblurs the previous map with SharpED, calculates phases by FFT from that deblurred map for every measured reflection (expanded over the full space-group symmetry), and recomposes a map from \|Fobs\| with those phases for the next cycle. Does not work well with some models.</li><br>                <li><b>SharpED (experimental)</b> &mdash; the same recycling loop, but skips Superflip entirely: cycle 1 starts from a map synthesized directly from \|Fobs\| with independent random phases. Not production-ready; can take hundreds of cycles to converge, if it converges at all.</li><br>            </ul><br>            <p>Both recycling methods are hidden unless <b>Show beta and experimental features</b> is checked on Advanced &rarr; Setup. Selecting one disables Next-cycle model, XPLOR damping, Symmetrize SharpED map and the per-cycle EDMA checkboxes below it, since the method defines its own next-cycle map; use <b>Run EDMA on final map</b> (Optional processing) instead. Each completed map is assessed with the active three-metric validation profile; the cycle-to-cycle map correlation remains available in the detailed output.</p><br>            <p><b>Next-cycle model</b> (Superflip phasing method only) is the authoritative source for cycle 2 onward: <b>None</b> forces a one-cycle run; <b>Superflip map (XPLOR)</b> cycles without SharpED processing; <b>SharpED map (XPLOR)</b> uses the SharpED-processed XPLOR map; <b>SharpED structure (EDMA CIF)</b> uses the EDMA structure extracted from the SharpED map and ignores XPLOR damping.</p><br>            <p><b>XPLOR damping (1/x)</b> (Superflip phasing method, XPLOR next-cycle models only) is the inverse damping factor: 1.0 means no damping, 0.5 is equivalent to the previous factor 2.0, 0.25 to factor 4.0.</p><br>            <p><b>Excluded atoms</b> removes selected atom labels from CIF modelfiles before the next Superflip cycle (comma/semicolon/whitespace-separated); it does not apply to XPLOR-only model paths.</p><br>            <h3>SharpED model</h3><br>            <p><b>Model</b> is the SharpED server model name sent with every deblurring request; <b>Refresh models</b> fetches the current list from the configured server and updates this selector. Server URL and API token are on Advanced &rarr; Setup; elements, output resolution, map value exponent and network/upload settings are on Advanced &rarr; SharpED.</p><br>            <h3>Optional processing</h3><br>            <p>Under <b>Superflip cycle</b> (used when Phasing method is Superflip):</p><br>            <ul><br>                <li><b>Run EDMA on Superflip map</b> &mdash; peak-search the Superflip XPLOR map and export CIF/XYZ/PDB.</li><br>                <li><b>Run SharpED</b> &mdash; process the Superflip map with the SharpED server. If disabled, the SharpED map used downstream is just a copy of the Superflip map.</li><br>                <li><b>Symmetrize SharpED map with Superflip (beta)</b> &mdash; after SharpED processing, run Superflip in symmetry mode (no charge flipping) with the SharpED map as modelfile, averaging it according to the supplied space-group symmetry. Hidden unless beta/experimental features are enabled.</li><br>                <li><b>Run EDMA on SharpED map</b> &mdash; peak-search the SharpED (or symmetrized) XPLOR map and export CIF/XYZ/PDB.</li><br>                <li><b>Compute OMIT validation maps (5% holdout)</b> &mdash; freezes a symmetry/Friedel-orbit-safe holdout before cycle 1 and excludes it from every work-data and feedback step. Enables the cross-validation assessment profiles and OMIT map correlation. Roughly doubles Superflip/SharpED time per cycle.</li><br>                <li><b>Calculate R_free on the 5% holdout</b> &mdash; is selected automatically with the option above and reports R_free from reflections never admitted to work data. Recommendation follows the active profile's explicit three-metric priority without a combined score.</li><br>            </ul><br>            <p>Under <b>Phase-recycling methods</b> (used by the two beta/experimental Phasing methods):</p><br>            <ul><li><b>Run EDMA on final map</b> &mdash; run EDMA once, on the last cycle's recomposed \|Fobs\|+phi_calc map.</li></ul><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| R-00370 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h3>Output</h3><br>            <p><b>Working directory</b> is where Phase Studio writes generated HKL files, Superflip inputs, maps, EDMA results, logs and metrics. Use a dedicated directory per run when reproducibility matters.</p><br>            <p><b>Map format</b> adds one extra saved output on top of the XPLOR map that is always kept internally for EDMA, SharpED and Superflip: <code>ccp4</code> or <code>jana</code> save an extra CCP4 map or Jana m80/m81 density+reflection files; <b>HKL reflections with phases</b> and <b>ShelX (fcf)</b> instead save, for each cycle's Superflip map, the observed reflections (h k l, intensity/F&sup2;, sigma) together with phases &mdash; and, for ShelX, calculated F&sup2; &mdash; read by FFT from that map, as a standardized text file or a ShelX/Jana-compatible .fcf file, in place of an extra density map.</p><br>            <p><b>Structure format</b> adds one extra saved structure format on top of the CIF that is always kept internally for metrics and next-cycle modelfiles: <code>xyz</code> or <code>pdb</code>.</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00371 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QToolButton text | ↑ Contents | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00372 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QGroupBox title | Map feedback reference | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00373 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <p>Each of the three mechanisms below has its own <b>Enable</b> checkbox at the top of its group; unchecking it grays out the rest of that group and skips the mechanism entirely. <b>Start after cycle</b> keeps its own 1-999 range regardless of the current <b>Cycles</b> value (Basic &rarr; Workflow), so it can be set up ahead of raising Cycles; the fields below it stay grayed out with a hint whenever the current Cycles value cannot reach the configured starting cycle.</p><br>            <h3>Missing-reflection completion</h3><br>            <p><b>Start after cycle</b> is the first completed cycle whose map is used to add missing reflections for the next cycle. <b>Maximum added reflections (%)</b> caps generated missing reflections as a percent of the current reflection count, preventing feedback from overwhelming measured data.</p><br>            <h3>Intensity correction</h3><br>            <p><b>Start after cycle</b> is the first completed cycle whose map is used to damp observed intensities for the next cycle. <b>Correction damping</b> ranges from 0 (keeps observed values) to 1 (replaces them with scaled map-derived values). <b>I/&sigma;(I) limit</b> selects positive observations strictly below the intensity-significance threshold; amplitudes use the first-order approximation F/[2&sigma;(F)], not recovered experimental intensity significance. Zero bypasses significance filtering for all map-supported reflections. The average intensity change across corrected reflections is plotted on the <b>Intensity correction</b> convergence tab (lower is better).</p><br>            <h3>Powder overlap repartitioning</h3><br>            <p>Only applies to reflections carrying an FWHM value (h k l · I · FWHM or h k l · F · FWHM). <b>Start after cycle</b> is the first completed cycle whose map is used to redistribute overlapping reflections for the next cycle. Reflections whose Bragg peaks overlap in a powder pattern -- delta(2θ) below <b>Separation factor</b> times the mean of their FWHM, Superflip's own <code>fwhmseparation</code> convention -- have their combined observed intensity redistributed between them using intensities calculated by FFT from that cycle's map (the SharpED map when SharpED deblurring is enabled, otherwise a copy of the Superflip map), blended by <b>Map ratio mix</b> (0 keeps the observed split, 1 uses the map split fully; default 1). The group total is always conserved. <b>Wavelength</b> is required to compute 2θ; if left at 0 it is auto-detected first from the loaded <code>.inflip</code> file's <code>lambda</code>/<code>wavelength</code> line, then from the reference file's <code>_diffrn_radiation_wavelength</code> tag -- enter it manually if neither source has it. Each time it runs, a <code>cycle_NNN_powder_repartitioning.log</code> file is written with the number of overlap groups considered, their average size, their average intensity change, and the before/after intensities for every reflection in the 3 groups with the largest d-spacing. The average intensity change per group is also plotted on the <b>Powder repartitioning</b> convergence tab (lower is better).</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
| R-00374 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <b>Warning</b><br>Missing-reflection completion and intensity correction rewrite the observed HKL data fed into later cycles, not just the model. Review the reconstruction carefully before trusting downstream cycles. | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |

Do not replace the scientific Superflip executable.</p><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |

| R-00380 | Shared main window / standalone fixture | QLabel text | <br>            <h2>Phase Studio</h2><br>            <p>Phase Studio is a crystallographic reconstruction workflow integrating Superflip, SharpED and EDMA with Jana2020-oriented workflows.</p><br>            <h3>Developed at</h3><br>            <p>Department of Structure Analysis<br>Institute of Physics of the Czech Academy of Sciences</p><br>            <h3>Phase Studio author and lead developer</h3><br>            <p><b>Jiří Zelenka</b><br><a href="mailto:zelenka@fzu.cz">zelenka@fzu.cz</a></p><br>            <p>Fyzikální ústav AV ČR, v. v. i. (Institute of Physics of the Czech Academy of Sciences).</p><br>            <p>Phase Studio is free open-source software under the MIT license. Scientific publications reporting work performed with Phase Studio should cite Jiří Zelenka, Phase Studio, version 2.0.7, <a href="https://github.com/ji-ze/Phase-Studio">the software repository</a>. Citation is the project's scientific-attribution policy, separate from the MIT license.</p><br>            <h3>SharpED developers — separate hosted service</h3><br>            <p><b>Jiří Zelenka</b></p><br>            <p><b>Jan Rohlíček</b><br><a href="mailto:rohlicek@fzu.cz">rohlicek@fzu.cz</a></p><br>            <p><b>Monika Kučeráková</b></p><p><b>Zdeněk Buk</b></p><br>            <p>SharpED account, personal API token and the applicable access entitlement/service terms are required for inference. A free Phase Studio license does not include a SharpED subscription or free inference entitlement.</p><br>            <p><b>General contact</b><br><a href="mailto:sharped@fzu.cz">sharped@fzu.cz</a></p><br>            <h3>Project resources</h3><br>         | See corresponding static implementation | Not verified | Pending individual review |
